# Joint ordinary/effect learning pilot, 2026-10-15

The frozen source checkpoint has `card_text=False`, `effect_text=False` and no card facts. It represents identities and public structured state but has no explicit text channel. The prior numeric residual failed all four effect challenge families; source was also weak. This pilot tests whether balanced native supervision plus public effect semantics can cover both ordinary and exceptional battles.

## Fixed protocol

Freeze the source trunk/head. Train five matched scalar logit residuals: numeric only; numeric plus contextual attacker/target embeddings; pair/numeric plus original public card text; pair/numeric plus static structured script text; and both text sources plus pair/numeric. All use the same 3340-input/128-hidden ReLU head with zero final layer; unused blocks are zero. Normalize from training inputs only.

Both text sources use the same frozen, locally cached E5-base-v2 revision `f52bf8ec8c7124536f0efb74aca902b2995e5bcd`. Encode token windows of 480 tokens with stride 448, passage prefix, normalized chunk mean, then renormalize; archive documents, hashes and chunk counts. Nothing is silently truncated. Raw text contains the public name/description. Structured text contains effect setter records with enclosing function and exact token expressions plus the entire comment-free canonical script. Generic setter labels/constants are expanded for readability; retain condition callbacks, target restrictions, scope, timing, clones and registration. No outcome-derived descriptions, family labels or dynamic effect flags. External helpers remain symbolic and missing scripts explicit: this is a conservative static code representation, not a complete Lua interpreter.

The protocol was expanded to these five arms at the user’s request before any text encoding, fitting or new evaluation. Script structure being easier to learn is a hypothesis, not an established result.

Reuse 109 ordinary native training prompts and 24 challenge prompts for immunity, reflection and piercing. Draw 16 ordinary and 16 effect examples per step, two seeds, 128 Adam steps at 0.001, fixed final endpoint, save every 32 steps. The entire damage-step stat-change family is excluded from fitting and normalization. No production-model or RL changes.

After all ten endpoints are frozen, collect 40 new roots/80 branches: 12 known-effect roots with changed stats, 12 new-card roots (Marshmallon, Daigusto Sphreez, Lancer Dragonute), eight withheld-family Cat’s Ear Tribe roots, eight ordinary controls. Repeat 16 branches cold. Independently specified fixture outcomes must match the native engine. Unique immediate damage/destruction Pareto labels remain conditional on the accepted attack; no strategic labels for cancel or tradeoffs. Daigusto Sphreez tests a combination of reflection and immunity.

Acceptance requires at least 75% in every effect subgroup and new ordinary panel, and at least 90% on the reused 50-prompt ordinary regression panel, in both seeds. Report newly introduced errors separately. All thresholds and inputs are fixed before fitting; no adaptive extensions or best-checkpoint selection. A failed gate is a valid result and blocks promotion. Passing only permits independent behavior confirmation.

Heldout means excluded from this supervised fit, not necessarily unseen by source pretraining or the text encoder. These constructed, mirrored roots are correlated; no frequency or win-rate inference follows. The already exposed Cat’s Ear Tribe identity is a withheld training family, not a blind discovery claim.

Evidence: `out/research/battle-context-joint-learning-2026-10-15/`.

## Execution provenance

The first launch stopped before fitting because the old challenge uses `reflect`/`pierce` category names rather than `reflection`/`piercing`. Its STOP and logs remain intact. The restart corrects only the input selector and verifies 109 ordinary training prompts, 24 effect training prompts and 50 reused ordinary regression prompts before launching. Protocol, vectors and fixture specifications are unchanged.

Restart evidence: `out/research/battle-context-joint-learning-restart-2026-10-15/`. The encoder runs locally in evaluation mode; both complete text documents and vector hashes are archived. The static-script helper has tests for retaining condition callbacks, clones and registrations, and for treating missing scripts as unknown rather than as evidence of no effects.

## Results

All ten fixed endpoints completed. The native engine verified 80 branches and 16 cold replays. The final report originally used `identity_sha256` where the monitor expects `study_sha256`; `validate_report.py` independently rechecked every input hash, every endpoint hash, native health and summary counts, and wrote `validated-report.json`. Original report/configuration and the first run’s STOP remain preserved. No results, labels or checkpoints were changed by this serialization adapter.

Correct target choices below are seed 2026101511 / seed 2026101512, except the single frozen source. These are conditional two-target decisions, not full-game win rates.

| Input arm | Effect panels (32) | New ordinary (8) | Reused ordinary regression (50) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Frozen source | 13 | 6 | 30 |
| Numeric | 24 / 23 | 8 / 8 | 39 / 40 |
| Numeric + contextual pair | 17 / 13 | 5 / 4 | 33 / 35 |
| Pair/numeric + original text | 18 / 19 | 8 / 8 | 36 / 33 |
| Pair/numeric + structured script text | **28 / 28** | 7 / 8 | 38 / 35 |
| Pair/numeric + both text sources | 24 / 24 | 8 / 8 | 33 / 30 |

The script-only text arm outperforms original prose on this small fixed panel. Across the two reflection panels it gets 8/8 per seed, compared with raw text’s 4/8; on the withheld damage-step family it gets 8/8 versus raw text’s 4/8. However numeric-only also achieves 8/8 and 7/8 on the withheld family, so that result alone does not demonstrate rule comprehension. Script text still gets only 2/4 in **each** piercing panel in both seeds; concatenating both sources gets 0/4 in both piercing panels. Source-correct effect choices become wrong twice per seed for both script and combined text.

Every arm fails the preregistered joint gate. Script text’s 38/50 and 35/50 ordinary retention are below 45/50; it introduces six and eight errors on previously source-correct ordinary decisions. The three text arms all fit the 24 effect training prompts perfectly, so training accuracy does not settle generalization. Do not promote, scale or claim full-game improvement.

The next bounded investigation should distinguish text/feature interference from missing coverage: inspect the piercing failures and test a factorized representation (explicit attacker and target effect roles, applicability conditions, ordinary outcome component) with balanced counterexamples and an ordinary-retention objective. Original prose remains a useful complementary input candidate, but simple concatenation is not supported by this result. Any new fitting must use a new protocol and new evaluation roots; the present panel is now exposed development evidence.

## Post-hoc target-text role diagnostic

A separate, recorded protocol (`text-role-audit-protocol.json`) tested the now-exposed panel without fitting or changing endpoints. Hold source, numeric inputs, contextual pair and attacker text fixed; replace both candidate target-text vectors by their per-root mean. Replayed baseline logits match the original report. This artificial intervention removes target text identity differences; it is not a legal card substitution or independent evaluation. All 40 roots are archived, with interpretation focused on the eight piercing roots.

| Text arm | Piercing before (seed 1 / 2) | Equal target text (seed 1 / 2) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Original prose | 2 / 3 | 8 / 8 |
| Structured script | 4 / 4 | 8 / 8 |
| Both | 0 / 0 | 8 / 8 |

Both target cards in these piercing fixtures are ordinary monsters with missing individual scripts; their script documents have identical boilerplate and differ only in card name. Thus the script channel’s target-name difference materially affects these failures despite the unchanged attacker’s piercing semantics and unchanged stats. The diagnostic supports prioritizing an **identity-separated structured effect representation** and explicit attacker/target roles in the next bounded ablation. It does not show that equalizing target text is safe for reflection or immunity (where different target effects matter), and it does not repair ordinary retention. Original joint-gate failures stand.

## Identity preservation and non-battle transfer

User steering: card names and archetype information may be essential and must remain available. The diagnostic does **not** justify deleting identity. Keep password/alias, public name and archetype membership in an identity channel; keep effect applicability, costs, timing, target predicates and operations in a separate rule channel. A cosmetic card-name prefix may be ablated within the rule channel while the identity channel remains intact. Preserve semantic references such as `IsCode`, `IsOriginalCode`, `IsSetCard`, listed names and material requirements. Dynamic name/archetype changes require public state and engine semantics; static database membership alone is not definitive in those cases.

Concrete example: Runick Tip (`c31562086.lua`) filters `IsSetCard(SET_RUNICK) and not IsCode(id) and IsAbleToHand()`. A representation must preserve archetype membership **and** the named-card exclusion **and** the availability condition. Dropping card references or treating every Runick card as interchangeable would be wrong. Its `Effect.CreateMysteruneQPEffect` helper also illustrates why unexpanded helper calls must remain explicitly unresolved rather than treated as a complete effect specification: `cards_specific_functions.lua` supplies the alternate extra-deck summon mode, activation/count limits, banishing and the next-Battle-Phase skip. These are not recoverable from the individual card’s setter index alone. Original card prose remains a complementary source and a check against incomplete script extraction.

The repository already has optional card-fact inputs for archetype membership, referenced archetypes and action/location queries (`docs/nets.md`, `CardIdentity`, `build_card_facts.py`). Membership and reference embeddings share a table. This is useful infrastructure, not evidence that the source checkpoint uses it (it has `card_facts=False`) or that the coarse facts preserve complete predicates: full negation, named exceptions, sub-archetypes and dynamic restrictions need separate verification.

The representation can describe non-battle effects; the present fitted residual only scores accepted-attack targets. Transfer to the general policy requires wiring the representation into general card/effect/action features and training with non-battle examples. No result here establishes such behavioral transfer.

Next validation design, before any broader retraining:

1. **Search/recovery:** archetype membership, exact named-card exclusions, source/destination locations and negation; include same-archetype hard negatives and named exceptions.
2. **Summon/material selection:** explicit card references, type/level/archetype restrictions and counts; verify complete combinations with the native engine, not just individual material membership.
3. **Activation/cost/targeting:** matched public states differing in payable cost, timing or target applicability, with the same card identities. Static declarations must not stand in for active effects.

Compare raw text, structured rules, and both using matched identity channels, parameter/update budgets, and native outcomes. Separate within-archetype new-card transfer from whole-archetype holdout; also split effect templates and state conditions to prevent near-duplicate leakage. Rule applicability probes must not receive the engine’s legality label through an action mask. Conversely, improved applicability classification alone does not prove policy gains, since the live engine already supplies legal actions: subsequent behavioral validation must compare choices among legal actions and their native continuations. Retain ordinary-battle and effect-exception regressions in the joint gate.

Before a non-battle learning canary, audit shared-helper coverage and parameter binding; fail coverage validation when required callback/condition dependencies are unresolved. The existing filter IR intentionally over-approximates unknown predicates and treats some state-dependent predicates as action hints, so it must not be promoted to an exact legality oracle. Native validation must include positive and negative applicability cases.

## Executed non-battle prerequisite audits

Two bounded studies now provide native fixtures and concrete extraction requirements. Both used the unchanged official card scripts and the frozen `codex-explicit-battle-context` runtime, bound the environment/input hashes, checked engine health, completed under monitored user services, and were acknowledged after report verification. No policy was trained or promoted.

1. `out/research/effect-text-nonbattle-audit-2026-10-15/`: **24/24 cases and 24/24 cold replays pass** (10 positive, 14 negative). Runick Tip, Branded Retribution and Reinforcement of the Army cover same-name exclusion, archetype membership, source-location errors, level/race boundaries, and the Runick helper's alternate extra-deck summon. Positive cases check actual destination zones, Retribution's self-banish cost, and Runick's opponent banishment and once-per-turn suppression. Both seats are covered. Development preflight first exposed missing fixture placement responses; the original runner/results remain archived, and deterministic placement/position handling was fixed before freezing. Expected labels were unchanged.
2. `out/research/effect-text-state-gates-2026-10-15/`: **12/12 cases and 12/12 cold replays pass** (8 positive, 4 negative). Within each pair all card identities and seeds are held fixed; only the controller's Imperial Iron Wall changes between face-down and face-up. Active Iron Wall prevents Runick's search activation because the required opponent banishment is unavailable, and prevents Retribution's graveyard activation because its self-banish cost cannot be paid. ROTA remains usable in both states. The own face-down identity is known to its controller; this does not authorize revealing opponent private cards.

The coverage artifacts preserve public names, passwords/aliases/setcodes, raw card text, exact canonical scripts, and positional bindings into the Runick closure factory. The second audit archives six reachable shared definitions in the two indexed libraries: `Effect.CreateMysteruneQPEffect`, `Cost.SelfBanish`, `Auxiliary.SelectUnselectGroup`, `Auxiliary.Stringid`, `Duel.SelectEffect`, and `Auxiliary.SelectUnselectLoop`. The entire Runick factory body retains its local closures. References to native APIs and unresolved methods remain explicit.

These results demonstrate that the fixtures distinguish identity, rule and state requirements. They **do not** demonstrate that a learned representation handles them. Named dependency closure within two files is also not full semantic coverage: lexical captures, callback binding, object methods, aliases and imports still require a conservative extraction contract. Both reports explicitly leave full coverage/learning readiness false. The immediate remaining task is to validate that contract before the matched original-text/structured-text/both learning comparison; subsequent evaluation must use new cards/archetypes and retain the battle regression gates.

## Executed callback extraction and non-battle text pilot

`out/research/effect-text-transfer-pilot-2026-10-15/` completes the remaining scoped prerequisite and the four-arm learning comparison. Its identity hash is `10566c057e5266d3d41f545dbe0700bf8afaa9a6b21417f16055c29698090e44`. The report, frozen inputs, prepared artifacts and eight endpoint hashes were independently rechecked before acknowledging completion. Process health passed; **every learning arm failed its generalization gate**. These are separate outcomes.

The research extractor indexes callbacks, formal parameters and positional bindings, traverses shared dotted functions and methods, resolves aliases, and retains entire closure factories. Fourteen shared symbols are reconstructed from canonical source after clearing their originals. `Card.RegisterEffect` requires capturing the original native binding *before* utility loading, then applying the extracted wrapper once; otherwise the closure can capture a missing or already-wrapped function. `Group.Iter` is restored after its `Auxiliary.Next` referent. Native APIs are explicitly identified from the pinned native source declarations and remain runtime terminals.

All **192 official-versus-extracted cases agree**, including native decisions and positive resolutions; **24 cold replays agree**. Removing `Cost.SelfBanish` or `Duel.SelectEffect` intentionally is detected in both negative controls. This closes the executable extraction gate for seven selected source/restriction cards and these constructed cases. It does not establish arbitrary Lua equivalence or complete extraction for every card. The original prerequisite reports remain unchanged.

The learning task is binary **activation applicability**, not target-choice quality or full-game play. Four source effects (Runick Tip, Branded Retribution, Reinforcement of the Army and Fossil Dig) supply 64 training cases. Each source/candidate pair is a four-state quartet: candidate in deck/graveyard crossed with own Imperial Iron Wall face-down/face-up. Evaluation uses 64 new-candidate cases, 32 E - Emergency Call cases with the entire HERO example family excluded from fitting, and 32 Summoner's Art cases with its normal-monster rule excluded from fitting. The last split may reuse candidate identities. There are only 16 training pairs and correlated state variants, not 64 independent card combinations.

All arms retain the same source/candidate password, alias, public name, archetype membership, numeric facts and observable state. No legality mask, action list or native outcome appears in the model inputs. Raw and structured rule blocks describe the source effect and restriction card; candidate rule text is omitted consistently in all arms. Structured documents include canonical code and the dependency/callback index, rather than a verified high-level symbolic interpreter. All text uses the same frozen local E5 encoder and complete token windows. The 4751-input/64-hidden MLP, initialization, balanced minibatch draws, 128 Adam updates and fixed final endpoint are matched across four arms and two seeds. Normalization uses training rows only.

Balanced accuracy below is seed 2026101561 / 2026101562. The preregistered gate requires balanced accuracy, positive recall and specificity each at least 75% in every evaluation split in both seeds, with no balanced-accuracy regression from the identity/state baseline.

| Input arm | New candidates (64) | Unseen HERO effect (32) | Unseen normal-monster rule (32) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Identity + state | 50.0 / 50.0% | 62.5 / 62.5% | 66.7 / 60.4% |
| Plus original text | 50.0 / 50.0% | 41.7 / 52.1% | 33.3 / 39.6% |
| Plus structured text | 50.0 / 50.0% | 56.3 / 62.5% | 50.0 / 43.8% |
| Plus both | 58.3 / 50.0% | 41.7 / 43.8% | 33.3 / 33.3% |

On new candidates, seven of eight endpoints predict all 64 cases unavailable: 52 true negatives and **12 false negatives**, giving a misleading ordinary accuracy of 81.25% despite zero positive recall. The combined arm's first seed recovers only 2/12 positives. Structured text does better than original prose on the two unseen-effect panels here, but does not outperform the shared baseline or meet the gate. No arm is promoted; this does not support broader retraining yet.

Training balanced accuracy itself is only 75.6–87.5%, so the failed transfer result cannot be attributed solely to representation quality or unseen-card generalization. A post-hoc, no-fit audit in `out/research/effect-text-transfer-diagnostic-2026-10-15/` reproduces all eight original logit arrays and verifies frozen hashes. Of the eight training quartets containing both positive and negative states, each endpoint gets only 0–2 quartets entirely correct. Most correctly order positives above negatives within a quartet (6–8 of eight), so state sensitivity is present but does not reliably yield the correct classification threshold across cards.

The same audit finds normalized factual/state feature mean L2 norms of 5.07 in training versus 11.41 on new candidates and 12.38 on the unseen normal rule. Binary categories absent in training become magnitude 20 when first present under the 0.05 standard-deviation floor. This is an input-distribution concern, not a proven unique cause of failure. The restriction card text is constant across this dataset and centers to zero; only its state changes. This pilot therefore cannot establish transfer to unseen restriction cards.

Before a new generalization comparison, separate optimization adequacy from representation changes. The next diagnostic is restricted to the same 64 training rows: reproduce each original 128-update endpoint, then inspect fixed 256/512/1024-update training results with no heldout inference or checkpoint selection. Any revised categorical scaling or factorized identity/rule/state architecture needs a separate protocol and fresh evaluation; the present panels are now exposed development evidence. Applicability success would still require subsequent legal-action choice and battle-retention validation.

The training-only check has now completed in `out/research/effect-text-fit-sanity-2026-10-15/`. It reproduces all eight original 128-update parameter sets before continuing the same optimizer/batch streams to fixed 1024 updates. At that final point, seven endpoints correctly classify all 64 training states; original-text seed 1 gets 62/64 (balanced accuracy 98.1%, two false positives). Both structured-text seeds fit perfectly. This establishes that the original short optimization budget was insufficient to fit the training fixtures. It does **not** repair or supersede the failed heldout gate: this diagnostic performs no heldout inference. A fresh-card, matched-budget comparison is required before judging transfer.

## Fresh-card optimization and normalization confirmation

`out/research/effect-text-transfer-confirmation-2026-10-15/` preregisters a bounded follow-up. Keep exactly the original 64 training rows; test 16 new candidate identities (64 state variants), disjoint from **all** original pilot identities. Compare the same four input arms and two seeds at fixed 1024 updates under two normalizations: original train-only standardization, or the same means/continuous/text scaling with binary feature standard deviations fixed at one. The latter isolates binary amplification; it does not remove identity or replace the rule input with hand-written applicability labels. All 16 endpoints are frozen before collecting fresh outcomes. Each fresh fixture requires official/extracted parity, with eight cold replays. Runtime cap: 900 seconds, no automatic restart.

The gate requires training balanced accuracy at least 95%, fresh balanced accuracy/positive recall/specificity each at least 75% in both seeds, and no fresh balanced-accuracy regression from the same-normalization identity/state arm. This panel tests new cards under the four already-trained effects; it makes no unseen-effect or full-game claim. Passing only permits further independent validation. Study identity: `7064f5cf5592a4061d2f7e46776a3d0caed950bb216b2bd5c0077722c5ab2c55`.

The confirmation completed successfully: **64/64 official/extracted cases and 8/8 cold replays agree**, all 16 endpoints and input hashes verify, and the monitored service exited successfully. All eight arm/normalization gates fail. Fresh balanced accuracy is:

| Input arm | Original normalization (seed 1 / 2) | Binary unit standard deviation (seed 1 / 2) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Identity + state | 72.1 / 60.6% | 52.2 / 49.0% |
| Plus original text | 72.1 / 72.1% | 61.5 / 49.0% |
| Plus structured text | 74.0 / 72.1% | 69.9 / 49.0% |
| Plus both | 78.2 / 67.9% | 62.8 / 58.3% |

Under original normalization, structured text recalls 6/12 fresh positives in each seed (specificity 51/52 and 49/52); original text also recalls 6/12 in each seed. Combined text recalls 7/12 and 5/12. These remain below the required 9/12 positives. The structured arm is weakly better than the baseline on this particular panel, but the result does not establish a reliable representation advantage. The original-normalization endpoints fit training almost perfectly (seven 64/64, raw seed 1 at 62/64).

Fixing binary standard deviations at one does not rescue transfer and reduces balanced accuracy for every matched endpoint here. The combined-text arm also falls below the training adequacy threshold (94.2% and 89.7% balanced accuracy), so equal optimization budgets are not equal convergence across these scalings. This rejects the simple claim that preventing binary amplification alone fixes the observed failures; it does not show that magnitude-20 categories are generally desirable. The original and confirmation panels have different candidate identities, so their aggregate differences are not a paired estimate of the benefit of extra optimization. The training-only reproduction provides the direct evidence for underfitting.

The immediate scientific conclusion is scoped: executable callback/dependency reconstruction works on the tested fixtures; learning complete code/prose embeddings with a small concatenation head has not demonstrated dependable non-battle applicability transfer. Prioritize an explicit condition-to-candidate/state interaction control before broader sampling or policy retraining. Such a control must preserve named/archetype predicates and unknown conditions, compare against the learned text arms with adequate fitting, and use fresh cases; an interpreter supplied exact predicates would be an oracle diagnostic, not a fair claim that text learning improved. No production-policy changes or promotion follow from these studies.

## Condition interaction experiment design

The next bounded study separates three observed mechanisms: identity/archetype matching, condition applicability, and irrelevant state restrictions. In the fresh confirmation, structured text misses one Runick search, both Branded recoveries, and three Fossil Dig states; both structured seeds have the same six false negatives. In particular, flipping Iron Wall changes Fossil Dig scores although the engine still permits the same search. This is a controlled state-response error; attributing every miss to text comprehension would be premature.

Before further fitting, implement a conservative **scoped predicate compiler** for the six selected search/recovery effects. Parse the exact target-filter conjunction into set membership, race, type, level bounds and named exclusion; bind its target zone and the selected effect's banishment prerequisite from the already-validated shared helper/cost. Keep clause-level provenance and reject unknown predicates. `IsAbleToHand` and the absence of other restrictions are explicitly fixture assumptions, not generally true card properties. Static set membership is valid only in these unmodified constructed states. The compiler is a rule-assisted probe, not a replacement for engine legality or a general Lua interpreter.

Train on the same 64 rows. Compare original prose, full structured-script embedding, both, structured plus exact archetype relation, structured plus eight separate predicate-satisfaction values, and a shared clause-pooling classifier over those values. Preserve identity in all concatenation arms; the clause classifier receives exact identity/archetype relationships through compiled predicates. Its smaller shared architecture is an intentional inductive-bias control, not a parameter-matched text model. No overall legality bit is fed to any learned head. The conjunction of predicates is reported separately as a scoped oracle diagnostic, never credited as learned text understanding.

Use two fixed seeds and 2048 updates per arm, no selection. Evaluate 128 fresh states from 32 candidate identities excluded from both previous panels: 64 under trained source effects, 32 under the heldout Elemental HERO rule, and 32 under the heldout normal-monster rule. All native outcomes are collected only after endpoints freeze; include official/extracted parity and cold replays. Gates retain >=95% training balanced accuracy, >=75% evaluation balanced accuracy/recall/specificity in each split and both seeds, plus no regression versus the matched structured-text baseline. Passing this constructed applicability probe only permits independent scope/behavior validation; it does not permit policy promotion or broad retraining.

## Condition interaction results

The study completed with **128/128 official-versus-extracted cases**, **16/16 cold replays**, and **128/128 scoped predicate-oracle checks** passing. All input, prepared-artifact and endpoint hashes verify; the service exited successfully and its completion event was acknowledged. Study identity: `fa0ba129291dfa08d5058d18de1c1ad81f9cc7dd5e5a83535dda97a0360aaec7`. All twelve models classify the 64 training states perfectly, so this comparison is not limited by the original training underfit. The reusable scoped compiler is `tools/effect_condition_probe.py`; eight compiler/guard tests and the two existing text-renderer tests pass.

Balanced accuracy is seed 2026101561 / 2026101562:

| Arm | Fresh candidates (64) | Heldout HERO effect (32) | Heldout normal-monster effect (32) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Original text | 73.1 / 73.1% | 81.2 / 75.0% | 83.3 / 68.8% |
| Structured script text | 72.1 / 77.2% | 75.0 / 81.2% | 77.1 / 77.1% |
| Both text sources | 73.1 / 73.1% | 81.2 / 81.2% | 79.2 / 77.1% |
| Structured + exact archetype relation | 77.2 / 77.2% | 75.0 / 75.0% | 79.2 / 68.8% |
| Structured + eight condition values | 83.7 / 83.7% | 83.3 / 83.3% | 83.3 / 83.3% |
| Shared classifier over condition values | 100.0 / 100.0% | 100.0 / 100.0% | 100.0 / 100.0% |

Only the shared clause classifier passes the full gate, with **128/128 correct in both seeds**, including all 28 positives and 100 negatives. Adding only exact archetype membership is insufficient. Adding all clause values to the original concatenation head improves fresh-candidate recall to 9/12 in both seeds, but still yields eight false positives in each unseen-effect panel (specificity 16/24), failing the joint gate. Thus providing the condition values and composing them appropriately are distinct interventions.

Inspection of the concatenation arm's errors is specific: its eight HERO false positives per seed are Destiny/Vision HERO candidates that fail the Elemental HERO set predicate. Its eight normal-rule false positives per seed violate minimum level and/or Normal type. The input already contains these failed-condition values, yet this architecture does not apply them reliably in the unseen effect context. The shared classifier instead uses the same function for every clause, then pools the clause representations. It supplies a strong compositional prior and has fewer parameters; its improvement is not evidence that a larger text model learned these semantics unaided.

The earlier six structured-text false negatives were identical across seeds: Runick Flashing Fire, both new Branded cards, and three Fossil Dig states. The recorded no-fit audit (`prior-error-audit.json`) also confirms that switching Iron Wall on turns an otherwise correct Uraby/Fossil Dig prediction into a false negative in each seed although both native states are legal. This supports a concrete irrelevant-state binding failure. New-card false negatives alone do not uniquely identify name memorization versus missing rule relationships. The new ablations support explicit binding and shared composition as a remedy **within this probe**, not as the established sole cause of every model error.

This is a **hybrid rule-assisted result**. The compiler evaluates exact script-derived predicate relations against known fixture facts; it is much stronger semantic input than an E5 embedding of complete Lua text. A separate conjunction oracle also agrees with all native labels. The learned pooling classifier merely learns to aggregate these conditions; it does not discover the card rules. Unsupported syntax and out-of-scope source cards are rejected. IsAbleToHand, dynamic names/types, count limits, other restrictions and general timing are not solved by this implementation. The explicit fixture-scope guard and native validation are essential.

There is also a direct limitation for policy improvement: all 28 legal-positive states have the same eight-condition vector and exactly identical pooled logits in each seed (`legal-choice-limitation.json`). The probe **cannot rank legal choices by their downstream utility**, and the live engine already supplies legal actions. Do not use this score as a policy value or claim win-rate gain. The next useful behavioral study must retain exact identity/rule/state bindings while representing the operation and its consequences, then compare choices among multiple legal targets with native continuations and battle-retention checks. Broader retraining and policy promotion remain unjustified by an applicability result alone.

## Legal target value pilot and label readiness (2026-10-15)

The authorized behavioral pilot is `out/research/effect-choice-value-pilot-2026-10-15`, identity `8725109d3729e3c717d951d0322834a12cfc89c250d0299f4857fed038e067ab`. Its frozen protocol compares utility among three already legal targets in 24 constructed states: six disjoint Warrior triples, Reinforcement of the Army versus The Warrior Returning Alive, and Blue-Eyes versus Spirit Reaper as opposing monster. Sixteen roots train; eight use disjoint candidate identities. Source effects, own Celtic Guardian and opponent identities are shared context, so this is not an unseen-context or source-effect split.

Each target is followed by the unchanged attack-history checkpoint against a greedy opponent under two paired suffix RNG seeds. Prefixes, legal menus and original-policy probabilities must match across branches; the selected card must reach hand, and engine health must be clean. The budget is 144 formal continuations plus eight training cold replays, at most eight turns/300 decisions per continuation. Terminal wins/losses score +2/-2; capped outcomes use LP difference/6000, explicitly a censored proxy. These are conditional fixed-policy values in selected fixtures, not production rewards or population win-rate estimates.

Before fitting or heldout collection, at least eight of sixteen training roots must have mean target-value range >=0.05, and at least eight must have a unique best target shared by both suffix seeds. Only if this passes does the protocol permit four equal-width heads (identity/state, raw text, structured code, both), two seeds and 512 fixed updates each. Names/archetypes, public numeric facts and candidate-to-board stat interactions are shared controls; the text arms include source, candidate, own monster and opponent descriptions. Structured input preserves callbacks, operation setters, reached helpers and C++ native aliases such as `Group.FromCards`. All endpoints would freeze before heldout native outcomes. Passing would require a >=0.05 regret reduction versus original-policy argmax and identity-only in both seeds without mean-utility regression in either opponent subset; any success would still require independent confirmation, not promotion.

The 96 training continuations and eight cold replays completed cleanly. The service then stopped at aggregation because all observed utilities were integers and `torch.tensor` inferred `Long`, which cannot be averaged without an explicit floating dtype. The STOP and preflight serialization-error evidence are preserved. The separately monitored `effect-choice-value-recovery-2026-10-15` reads the existing traces, validates health/prefixes/replays, and aggregates as float32; it does not rerun collection or alter the gate.

The recovered result is **4/16 informative roots and 4/16 stable unique optima**, below the required 8. All eight Spirit Reaper roots tie at terminal +2 for all targets. Four Blue-Eyes roots distinguish Exiled Force or D.D. Warrior Lady from alternatives; the other four tie at -2. Consequently **zero models were fitted and zero heldout continuations were collected**. This is insufficient training-label discrimination, not a negative result about raw or structured text. The finite-horizon LP proxy was never used because every branch terminated.

A bounded follow-up, `effect-choice-horizon-audit-2026-10-15`, replays twelve existing training branches from roots 1 and 13, preserving all original responses, actions, probabilities and final outcomes. It adds public LP and zone snapshots at every decision to distinguish trajectories that share a final win. It uses no fresh candidate outcomes, no fitting and no changed continuation policy; its independent service has a 300-second ceiling and watcher. Any subsequent objective/fixture change must receive a new protocol and cannot retroactively relax this pilot's gate. Broad strategic retention remains untested; no live policy weights or behavior are modified.

The horizon audit completed with **12/12 exact full-path replays**. In root 1, Exiled Force, Goblin Attack Force and Gearfried all win, but on turns 7, 3 and 5 respectively. In root 13, the three choices all win on turn 5, while opponent LP after the first battle turn differs (800, 200 and 500). Thus terminal label saturation hides measured tactical differences; this audit does not establish that a short-horizon objective is strategically sufficient.

A **new** preregistered study, `effect-choice-short-horizon-2026-10-15`, uses terminal outcome if ended by turn 3, otherwise LP difference/6000 at the first turn-4 decision before either agent acts. The native continuation still runs to the original eight-turn limit, and these later outcomes are separate retention checks, never primary training labels. This objective was chosen using the two training-root audits; the original pilot remains a failed readiness result. All training continuations must exactly match the original complete responses/actions/probabilities/final results. The panel, two suffix seeds, readiness thresholds, four representation arms and eight fixed fit endpoints remain unchanged. No heldout outcomes have been examined at registration. Text arms must pass the short-horizon gate **and** have no eight-turn mean-utility regression against either control in either board subset. The new job has the same 144-branch/2400-second limit and its own watcher. No broad training or live policy change is authorized by this diagnostic.

Short-horizon collection verified all 96 training paths against the original and passed readiness: **16/16 informative roots and 16/16 stable unique optima**. A text-preparation assertion then failed because canonical Lua tokens contain spaces around the dot in `Duel . SendtoHand`. The independently frozen `effect-choice-short-horizon-recovery-2026-10-15` (identity `19e9bfbeed02539b9be5d4bbf0dc2c487f452a3bc38db142bca8a70ecf2f98ec`) replaces this string check with parsed call inspection; all 16 relevant scripts and both source operations pass preflight. It reuses all training data and cold replays, changes no model/gate, and retains the original STOP. Its own service and watcher are running. No heldout outcomes or fitted endpoints existed at this recovery registration. The user subsequently authorized publication to PR #427.

Completion audit: `effect-choice-short-horizon-recovery-2026-10-15` finished healthily and was acknowledged. All 23,248 input hashes, 111 prepared artifacts and eight endpoints verify; all 144 native branches are healthy. The text gate fails for raw, structured and combined representations. Heldout mean regret (seeds 1573/1574) is 0/0 for identity plus numeric state, .0583/.1167 for raw, .0083/.1083 for structured and .0583/.1167 for combined, versus .1271 for original-policy argmax. This tiny panel does not establish an incremental text benefit. Exact endpoint inference was reproduced; only two of eight heads choose all 16 training optima, so equal budgets are not equal convergence.

The more serious issue is objective alignment: roots 16 and 18 have disjoint short- and long-horizon best-target sets. D.D. Assailant gives only .0167 short utility but wins; Amazoness Swords Woman gives .25 but loses. Native trace inspection shows Assailant incurs 1300LP damage while banishing itself and Blue-Eyes at turn2, then Celtic Guardian wins onturn7. Swords Woman reflects1500 damage but leaves Blue-Eyes alive and loses onturn6. The identity/state head attains every short-horizon optimum yet still loses these two roots. This rules out treating perfect proxy ranking as sufficient strategic performance. Two suffix seeds produce identical utility vectors, and source search/recovery variants repeat outcomes, limiting independence. These are consumed diagnostic cases, not fresh confirmation. Future work must preserve terminal preference, model action costs and zone consequences, and verify training adequacy before another unseen-panel comparison. No model was promoted, no large retraining was launched, and the user-authorized PR description has been updated with this evidence.


## Terminal-priority and transition-supervision pilot

`out/research/effect-choice-terminal-priority-2026-10-15/` freezes a bounded follow-up to the objective inversion. It reuses only the original 16 training roots/96 branches. Within each root, terminal outcome (+1 win/-1 loss) determines pairwise preference; short-horizon value breaks only exact terminal ties. The two pair sets receive separate losses, avoiding numerical dominance by abundant terminal ties. The learned ranking output is not a hard runtime guarantee. Censored outcomes never receive terminal labels.

All five arms share identity/archetype and public numeric input, the same 64-unit head, frozen E5 encoding and 1536 full-batch Adam updates. Compare identity/state, original prose, structured scripts and combined text with auxiliary transition supervision, plus structured text without that auxiliary loss; two fixed seeds give ten endpoints. Transition labels describe whether the original opposing threat remains on field or reaches GY/banishment, own zone-count changes, and both LP changes at the first own turn-3 decision. These future measurements are prediction targets only, never inference features. Own zone counts avoid confusing a selected Celtic Guardian with an existing copy.

The training adequacy gate requires every terminal-distinguishing pair correctly ranked, at least 95% of all non-tied pairs correct and terminal MSE <=.02 in both seeds. Only then can an eligible arm proceed to fresh confirmation. All endpoints and prepared inputs freeze first. Six fresh target identities (36088082,55821894,25652259,37043180,30860696,22609617), disjoint from prior target panels, yield eight roots and at most 48 native continuations plus four independent-process replays. Source/opponent context remains shared and roots correlated. Previously consumed evaluation roots are diagnostics only; they cannot select models or thresholds. Target-function tests use the known Assailant/Swords Woman traces to verify label semantics, not to fit weights.

The new gate requires no avoidable terminal errors and mean terminal value improvement >=.25 over frozen source argmax in both seeds, with no opponent-subset regression against identity/state. Short tie-break regret is reported separately. Any capped fresh continuation makes confirmation inconclusive. The protocol permits only 1800 seconds, no automatic restart or data extension, and no promotion or large retraining. Preflight passes terminal precedence, tie-breaking, censoring rejection, native consequence labels, source operation and dependency extraction for all 17 relevant scripts. Existing failure evidence and STOP files remain preserved.

Study identity: `553549fa7210ead16d591c6421f86b94a64f2d351503a3beb65d3bf18c540fb4`. The bounded service and independent watcher have started; encoding is complete and model fitting is underway.


Completion: all ten endpoints pass training adequacy, each ranking 48/48 non-tied pairs correctly including all eight terminal-distinguishing pairs. Terminal training MSE ranges from .0000049 to .0003691. All 48 fresh branches are healthy; four independent-process replays agree. Verification checks all 23,427 input hashes, seven prepared artifacts and ten endpoints; recomputed predictions match exactly.

The registered confirmation is **inconclusive**, because four D.D. Warrior branches (two source roots times two suffixes, against Blue-Eyes) hit the eight-turn cap. The engine returns `reason=turn_limit` with a proxy winner in one case; this is correctly rejected as a terminal label. At the last decision both boards and both hands are empty: D.D. Warrior and Blue-Eyes are banished while Celtic Guardian is in GY. These constructed fixtures disable draws, so additional turns alone do not restore an offensive resource. This is a fixture limitation and a reminder that removing a threat need not preserve a route to victory.

Posthoc censoring-aware diagnostics preserve the inconclusive gate. On the six fully observed roots, raw and combined text choose terminal-optimal targets in 6/6 roots in both seeds, versus 4/6 for source argmax, identity/state, structured text and structured without auxiliary supervision. The informative failures are the two Blue-Eyes roots of the new Bull Blader/Amazoness Fighter/Queen's Knight panel: raw/combined select the winning Bull Blader; the other learned arms choose the losing Amazoness Fighter. Every learned arm selects censored D.D. Warrior in the other two Blue-Eyes roots, which remains unknown, never imputed as a win or draw. Assigning these unknown target values their entire [-1,+1] range bounds raw/combined mean terminal value at [.5,1], versus source argmax at 0; this does not satisfy the stricter preregistered zero-regret/completeness gate. The six-root diagnostic is correlated and outcome-selected, not a new population estimate.

On the previously consumed eight-root panel, raw/combined also avoid both Assailant/Swords Woman inversions in both seeds; identity and both structured variants still miss them. This is supportive debugging evidence only. The present comparison cannot attribute any improvement uniquely to terminal supervision, auxiliary labels or additional optimization, because these changed together relative to the earlier short-horizon experiment. The structured auxiliary ablation does not improve its choices here. The next protocol must address natural draw/resource availability and evaluate genuinely fresh cases before any promotion; simply increasing the original cap or relabeling its truncated outcomes would not repair the test. No large retraining or policy replacement follows.


## Draw/resource control and matched objective factorial

`effect-choice-resource-audit-2026-10-15` isolates draw availability (zero/one card per turn) and offensive deck resources (twenty MST versus ten MST alternating with ten La Jinn). It uses only the four previously censored D.D. Warrior trajectories, unchanged frozen actor/opponent and eight-turn/300-decision cap. These remain constructed decks with no format-legality claim. All sixteen branches are healthy, four independent-process replays agree and the original no-draw/MST traces reproduce exactly; 23,505 input hashes verify. No-draw arms terminate 0/4; draw with MST terminates 2/4; draw with mixed resources terminates 4/4, all losses on turn 6. This repairs the observed resource-starvation fixture, not the policy. It does not establish that arbitrary future fixtures will all terminate. Identity: `e082fa57af2eea4343c613a6a9431dde221833f482077878ca987a2b419c585e`.

`effect-choice-objective-factorial-2026-10-15` preregisters sixteen matched fits: raw versus structured text, short-horizon versus terminal-priority supervision, auxiliary transition loss off/on, and two seeds. Every fit has identical architecture, initialization paired by seed, 1536 full-batch updates and learning rate .001. The objective factor includes the declared pair weighting and terminal regression, so it identifies a supervision package, not any individual loss coefficient. Transition supervision is labels only; no future states enter inference.

A design check found that all original training pair orders agree under short and terminal objectives. Therefore the new protocol explicitly promotes four already-consumed Assailant/Swords Woman/Dai Grepher roots to development training, making twenty roots/120 existing branches with two conflicting preference pairs. All arms receive these same data. These four roots permanently lose heldout status for this line of investigation. No new training rollout is needed, and no claimed improvement on these promoted examples counts as generalization.

The confirmation uses six new target identities: 64538655,21015833,66073051,23289281,48092532,35215622, disjoint from prior target panels. Two source effects and two boards yield eight correlated roots/48 native branches plus four cold-process replays. Only evaluation uses one-card per-turn drawing and mixed non-Warrior filler resources; original training trajectories remain untouched. Head inputs are unchanged and do not encode the new fixed deck/draw regimen, making this an explicit new-card and context-shift robustness test, not learned deck planning. At least one matched condition must fit its own objective adequately in both seeds before collection; every endpoint freezes before the first fresh outcome. All nineteen relevant scripts pass dependency preflight, and two promoted preference conflicts are verified.

All forty-eight branches must terminate natively, otherwise the result is inconclusive with no proxy-winner substitution. At least four roots must have terminal-value range >=.5 before a factor signal is considered. Matched terminal-minus-short and auxiliary-on-minus-off differences are reported per seed and opponent. A positive signal requires adequate fits, strictly positive terminal-value difference in both seeds and no opponent-subset regression. There is no automatic budget extension, panel replacement, promotion or broad retraining; the service is bounded at 1800 seconds and has its own watcher.

Factorial study identity: `efc193a362a44e5bfe85710c078855e727ddb909b83441e88a98de781bb956e2`. The study and independent watcher are running; fit endpoints are produced before native confirmation.


Factorial completion: all sixteen endpoints fit their respective objective perfectly (58/58 preference pairs each) and meet the regression thresholds. All 48 fresh branches are healthy; four independent-process replays agree. The audit verifies 23,511 input files, seven prepared artifacts and sixteen endpoints, with exactly reproduced predictions. Short-objective terminal heads and auxiliary-off consequence heads remain zero, verifying that their excluded supervision channels did not train those outputs.

One unselected Sasuke Samurai #4 continuation against Spirit Reaper reaches the eight-turn limit, so the preregistered full-panel result remains **inconclusive**. Unlike the previous no-draw deadlock, this case has normal draws and resource turnover, but still reaches the fixed cap. The four-case fixture audit therefore did not guarantee all future games would terminate. Do not change the old threshold or infer a loss from the engine's capped proxy winner.

Censoring-aware analysis makes the model comparison unambiguous despite that one missing counterfactual: no model selects the censored branch, and every selected terminal value is known. All sixteen learned models obtain mean terminal value 0, versus -.125 for source argmax. All matched objective and auxiliary contrasts are exactly zero on this panel. The unresolved candidate's two-seed mean is bounded by [0,1]; every model and the source choose another candidate valued at1 in that root. Thus it cannot change the observed selected values or their comparisons. These bounds do not retroactively pass the registered completeness gate.

Only two roots definitely distinguish target values; even completing the censored counterfactual can make at most three informative roots, below the preregistered four. In all four Blue-Eyes roots every candidate loses. On seven fully observed roots all learned heads are optimal in7/7 versus source6/7, but this is largely tied outcomes; bounds certify the eighth root optimal too and do not establish strong target-selection competence. The panel therefore provides no incremental benefit for terminal priority, auxiliary prediction or raw versus structured text. The fixed policy's downstream decisions and fixture discrimination require investigation before another ranking comparison; collecting more copies of these saturated states is unsupported. The original STOPs, reports and inconclusive conclusions remain intact, and no model is promoted.


## Continuation-policy control and development resource calibration

`effect-choice-continuation-causality-2026-10-15` changes only own post-resolution decisions to the existing greedy agent on six exposed factorial roots, keeping the source/target forcing, opponent, engine seed and resource fixture fixed. The original policy still observes actual history and consumes its usual sampling draws. Every intervention records both choices and the original probabilities. Four original-policy traces reproduce exactly; four greedy traces reproduce in independent processes. All 23,595 frozen input hashes verify. Identity: `38e03b2b10457fec04e7f8c7ead5b8ca40aac59a8bcb63433b1f685ab9627b1c`.

The 36 greedy counterfactuals contain **0/24 Blue-Eyes wins**, matching 0/24 original-policy wins. Spirit Reaper yields 11/12 greedy wins plus one censored trajectory, compared with 10/12 original-policy wins. Native health is clean. The preregistered Blue-Eyes loss-to-win trigger is absent, so prefix-isolation and fresh-seed followups correctly skip; actual budget is 44 trajectories including the eight baseline/replay checks. This negative control does not prove unwinnability: the stat-based greedy agent does not reason about effect-specific combat mechanics. It also does not establish a broad greedy improvement from the correlated Reaper subset. No learned policy changes follow.

The next bounded study, `effect-choice-resource-calibration-2026-10-15`, calibrates discrimination before another representation comparison. Only already-exposed training/development cards are used. Three fixed common filler compositions alternate ten MST with ten copies of Mokey Mokey (27288416, ATK300), Silver Fang (90357090, ATK1200), or La Jinn (97590747, ATK1800). Both players draw normally; source ROTA, opposing Blue-Eyes, own Celtic Guardian, initial LP, existing engine seeds, frozen continuation actor and eight-turn/300-decision cap remain fixed. Changing identity also changes DEF/race/attribute/level, so this is composition calibration, not isolated ATK causality. All three filler monsters are Normal and non-Warrior, preserving the three-target source menu.

Two calibration roots (original training panel0 and exposed development panel4) give 36 native branches plus six cold-process replays. A resource qualifies only if every branch terminates natively and each root contains a fixed target pair ordered strictly the same way under both suffix seeds. Select the highest-ATK qualifying composition by the registered rule. Only then automatically collect two additional original training roots (panels1/2), twelve branches plus two cold replays. Both must again terminate completely and contain a stable strict target pair. Maximum budget56, zero model fits, no cap extension or posthoc resource/seed search. These are development readiness checks, not fresh-card generalization. No population win-rate, deck-legality or policy-promotion claim is allowed.

Calibration identity: `5ac31acaaec398b5503024c589da48c3d77291a33e55c6440014b30808c599ce`. The fixed protocol and its conditional followup are running under a bounded service and independent completion monitor.


Calibration completed all56 trajectories:48 distinct branches and8 cold replays, all native-health checks clean;23,603 frozen input hashes verify. Low-resource composition has stable effect-versus-stat distinctions on both calibration roots: Exiled Force and D.D. Assailant each win2/2 while both alternatives lose2/2. Medium resources censor both Exiled Force suffixes but retain the Assailant distinction; high resources make all calibration choices lose. The low-resource selection therefore automatically advances. Of the additional roots, panel1 has three losing choices; panel2 distinguishes D.D. Warrior Lady (2wins) from Zombyra (2losses), with Dark Blade splitting1/1. Only1/2 additional roots is informative, so the registered readiness gate **fails**. The two medium-resource censored paths remain unknown. Do not retrofit a passing threshold; this study establishes composition sensitivity, not a ready broad test set.

`effect-choice-resource-seat-control-2026-10-15` then crosses own and opponent low/high resource composition independently on three exposed effect targets: Exiled Force, D.D. Assailant and D.D. Warrior Lady. Its registered budget is24 branches plus6 cold replays; only a consistent opponent-resource reversal in at least two cases enables four fixed new engine seeds on the first qualifying case (8 branches plus2 replays). Opponent resource identity remains hidden from the policy and is diagnostic metadata only. Exact root menu, prefix and policy probabilities must agree at fixed own composition across opponent worlds. Identity: `76645a2186a5be42f3732733f8eaf4c6fcc98aa647e04042d4210ae91c01b44d`.

The complete40-trajectory control confirms the reversal on **all three targets and both suffixes**, regardless of own resource composition: low opponent resources yield a win, high opponent resources a loss. Four new engine seeds reproduce this reversal4/4 for Exiled Force. All32 distinct trajectories terminate natively with clean health, eight cold replays agree, ten available diagonal baselines reproduce exactly and23,659 input hashes verify. The own-resource main effect and interaction are zero on these observed terminal values; the opponent-composition effect is -2 on the [-1,+1] scale. This is a selected constructed mechanism, not a general ATK law or population effect.

The effect is actually used: both low/high Exiled Force traces summon and activate it onturn1, removing Blue-Eyes. The subsequent opponent monster can still erase or outlast the remaining offensive resource. Thus successful rule use and threat removal are insufficient for a final win. Identical audited target-menu/prefix/logits under opposite native outcomes also rule out demanding perfect per-world terminal classification from these root signals. This is not proof that every internal input tensor is identical, nor does it invalidate deterministic prediction of **expected** value. A legitimate value function must average over unobserved futures under a specified distribution; actual hidden opponent composition must not be injected as an inference feature.

The automatically launched followup `effect-choice-public-value-audit-2026-10-15` qualifies such labels before another fit. It fixes a declared artificial50/50 mixture of the two opponent resource worlds and low own resources. Every world must terminate; all roots need a stable unique mixture-best target in both suffixes and value range>=.5. Only then complete alternative choices for the four predetermined Exiled Force confirmation seeds, reusing8 branches and collecting16 plus4 cold replays. Require the same unique mixture-optimum in all four seeds and mean margin>=.5. Maximum35 new trajectories, no fitting or policy changes. The50/50 distribution is an explicit synthetic prior, not an estimated belief about real opponents. The completion seeds were already exposed for the removal target; this is choice completion, not an independent repeat of that target's win/loss reversal.

## Fixed-mixture expected-value representation pilot

`effect-choice-mixture-fit-2026-10-15` begins a new, explicitly distributional hypothesis. Use the original nine mixture-label rows from roots0/8/16, including Dark Blade's original -.5 mean; do not overwrite that label with the subsequent diagnostic. Compare identity/state, raw text and structured script with identical 64-unit masked-input heads, two seeds and1536 Adam updates. A sigmoid head learns soft win-frequency targets (no native draws occur in these training data), with score mapped to [-1,+1]. The actual opponent-resource world is never an input. Own low-resource regimen and artificial50/50 opponent prior are fixed protocol context. Cached numeric/card-text rows must match exactly across their original manifests, and normalization uses only the nine training rows.

An arm needs every strict training-mean pair correct and maximum absolute expected-value error <=.05 in both seeds. Only if at least one arm passes does collection proceed. Freeze all six endpoints first, then evaluate Bull Blader/Amazoness Fighter/Queen's Knight and Sasuke Samurai #4/Hayabusa Knight/Warrior of Zera under the same resource mixture, two fixed engine seeds and four suffixes:48 branches plus4 cold replays. These target identities are absent from this fit but were historically exposed in prior studies. This is development transfer to new resource-conditioned combinations, **not** globally untouched card confirmation. The tiny training set and two correlated evaluation roots strongly limit conclusions.

The new criterion concerns mixture expectations rather than a unique optimum in every sampled continuation: all48 outcomes must terminate, both roots must have value range>=.5, and a text arm must reduce mean mixture regret by at least .25 against both identity and original-source argmax in both fit seeds, with no per-root regression. Identity and the text arm must both fit adequately. Earlier failed strict gates remain unchanged. This protocol is bounded at six fits/52 trajectories/1800seconds with an independent watcher, no automatic extensions and no promotion. Even a positive signal only supports a subsequent untouched-card/context confirmation.


Mixture-fit completion, identity `b43d7b8ecf92b3ce8063dfcc1d4b3bbde8949c2c0f578357581dd6350eab7a4e`: all six endpoints pass training adequacy (7/7 strict preference pairs each; maximum absolute value error .00034–.00702). All48 new native branches are healthy, four cold replays agree, 23,752 input hashes/two prepared artifacts/six endpoints verify, and inference reproduces exactly. Two Sasuke Samurai #4 continuations hit the original cap, so the registered full result remains **censored-development-transfer**. The original cap and completeness requirement remain intact.

A separate bounds audit still identifies every target preference. Bull Blader has mixture value0 while both alternatives have-1. Sasuke Samurai #4 is bounded by [-.75,-.25], strictly above both alternatives at-1, regardless of the two missing terminal outcomes. In both fit seeds, raw text chooses both optimal targets; structured script text chooses Queen's Knight incorrectly in the first root and Sasuke correctly in the second; identity/state chooses Queen's Knight and Hayabusa Knight, both incorrect. Original-source argmax matches the structured choices. Thus raw-minus-source and raw-minus-structured mean value are exactly+.5, and raw-minus-identity is bounded by[+.625,+.875]. These are within-panel differences, not population confidence intervals. Uncertainty cannot reverse this observed ranking, but it still blocks the stricter registered complete-data gate; the second root's value range is also not guaranteed to reach .5 under its entire bound.

This is a useful **raw-text development signal**, not support for the prior expectation that full structured Lua embeddings must be superior. All fits are adequate, and hidden worlds were kept out of inference. However, only nine training rows and two historically exposed evaluation roots were used. The sequence of resource diagnostics changes the evaluation distribution, so no claim is made that expected-value supervision alone caused the improvement over previous studies. The next meaningful confirmation must lock the mixture/context protocol and endpoints and use genuinely unconsumed candidate/context combinations, while separately testing whether a more focused script representation fixes the structured arm's Bull Blader error. Do not collect more copies of saturated high-resource roots or promote this nine-row probe.


The next preregistered control is `effect-choice-public-context-confirmation-2026-10-15`. Freeze the six mixture heads and their original normalization; do not fit again. Cross the two evaluation target triples with Gearfried and Goblin Attack Force as the visible own monster, replacing the previously constant Celtic Guardian. All these identities have appeared elsewhere, but own-role variation was absent from the preceding fit line. This is explicitly heldout own-role/context transfer, not globally novel card validation. Reconstruct the original schema exactly on all fifteen previous feature rows, then change only the visible own-card slot using existing cached embeddings. Actual hidden resource world remains excluded.

Four fixed new engine seeds, two fixed suffixes, three choices and two hidden-resource worlds give48 branches plus4 cold replays. Predictions freeze before the first outcome. Require all48 terminals, at least two informative roots (range>=.5), mean regret improvement>=.25 versus identity and source in both seeds, and no regression within either own-context subset. Censoring remains inconclusive. This52-trajectory, zero-fit followup tests whether the observed raw-text signal survives a visible context shift; it does not enlarge training or modify the main policy. Its service and independent watcher use a1200-second bound.


Context-confirmation completion, identity `cf8b4e1ec70766e7030e9bdb7081e1c81d8face1982f9f0d6092dc3c97b3ad1b`: all 48 native branches are healthy, four cold replays agree, 23,819 input hashes and both frozen prediction artifacts verify, and all six endpoints reproduce predictions exactly. Seven continuations reach the eight-turn cap, so the formal result remains **censored-context-confirmation**. The selected visible-context shift therefore does not confirm the preceding raw-text advantage.

In root order Gearfried/Bull-panel, Gearfried/Sasuke-panel, Goblin/Bull-panel, Goblin/Sasuke-panel, the three target-value bounds are: `([-1,0],-1,-1)`, `(-1,[-1,-.5],[-1,-.5])`, `([-1,0],-1,-1)`, and `([.5,1],-1,-1)`. Raw text chooses the first target in all four roots; structured and source argmax choose the third target in the Bull panels and first in the Sasuke panels. Identity chooses third/second/third/second. These choices agree across fit seeds. Raw-minus-source mean value is only bounded by [0,+.5], so a strictly positive advantage is not established. Raw-minus-identity is bounded by [+.25,+1], but the Gearfried subset can regress by as much as .25 on average, violating any claim of certified retention. Raw is certified weakly optimal in three roots, including two where every choice could tie at -1; this is not a 75% demonstrated improvement rate. The Gearfried/Sasuke root can make raw's first choice strictly worse than either alternative.

Censored traces still contain live monsters, draws and possible offensive resources; they are not all the earlier empty-board/no-draw deadlock. Therefore `effect-choice-context-horizon-audit-2026-10-15` preregisters a separate, outcome-selected horizon-sensitivity diagnostic: rerun only these seven paths at a fixed 32-turn/1200-decision limit, four already-completed controls (first lexicographic completed branch per root), and four fixed cold replays, at most 15 trajectories/1200seconds. Every extended path must preserve the entire original root, action prefix, response-byte prefix and decision-trajectory prefix; completed controls must match their full original artifacts exactly. No model is fitted or changed. Any remaining truncation stays unknown, with no further extension.

A worse strong-opponent terminal outcome is a terminal counterexample; absent that, a lower next-own-turn LP is only a local-cost counterexample. No observed counterexample cannot establish safety; censoring keeps terminal claims inconclusive. The shared pass creates a deliberate legal-action challenge and cannot retroactively pass the natural-retention study. This diagnostic informs state-conditional training targets; it does not promote a heuristic or a text model.

## Frozen greater-route transfer and loss-geometry diagnosis

`effect-resolution-greater-transfer-2026-10-15` completes 180 healthy native paths with zero fits. Independent audit verifies 28,600 hashes, 168 event/state reconciliations, four historical controls, eight cold replays, 40 LP/eight categorical models, and every score, gate and 32-core interval. Identity `df82897b1ca9a46d91f3bfbcd85676f44c204c6a90738d8bfdde27ecf3f4ec18`. All six attackers remain stronger than the new normal defenders. Hayabusa repair transfers: per-opponent LP MAE is 0.60–8.03. Deterministic/control categorical predictions remain correct and numerical nonregression checks pass. Sasuke succeeds in 12/32 independent cores, frequency .375 with 95% interval [.21875,.53125]; paired worlds/opponents do not increase sample size.

No representation passes both seeds. Remaining Bull per-opponent LP MAE is 29.56–32.05 for identity, 25.07–27.68 raw, 24.58–27.63 canonical, and 20.29–25.045 combined. Combined seed 1730 misses the 25 threshold at full precision; 1731 alone passes. Preserve both this failure and the previous development failure. Deterministic/control pooled LP MAE is 5.79–8.40, illustrating why pooled success cannot substitute for per-card checks. This does not establish numerical text superiority or main-policy improvement.

Preregister `effect-resolution-loss-geometry-2026-10-15` before any further fitting. Freeze eight Leaky40 and eight augmented46 endpoints, reproduce each original train/test output, and analyze all 16 on the exact same 46 training inputs and empirical targets. No new defender labels enter the gradient analysis. Bound to three analytic objectives, zero fits/native paths/parameter updates and 1200 seconds. The objectives are current normalized LP-MSE, two-recipient allocation-share MSE, and three-way allocation cross-entropy. For nonzero attack gaps derive the allocation target from existing empirical mean LP loss divided by the gap; the third component is undelivered damage. Validate nonnegative probabilities summing to one without inserting card rules or an analytical coin expectation. Clamp and renormalize only floating-point simplex roundoff within 1e-6, rejecting larger violations. Omit zero-gap rows from share losses and verify their LP gradient is zero.

Measure gap-squared weighting, per-row allocation/logit gradients, and six card-weighted parameter-gradient contributions. Store compact Gram matrices, cosine angles, norms and Bull-versus-rest signed dot products. Reconstruct analytic softmax derivatives independently against autograd; verify that card gradients sum to the full objective and chain-rule parameter gradients agree. Recheck all model tensors remain bit-identical. Negative Bull/rest dot products diagnose only local first-order conflict; report magnitude/zero-gradient flags rather than treating sign alone as evidence of a large problem. Different objective gradient magnitudes are not comparable evidence of efficacy. Any suggested loss change requires a separately preregistered matched training experiment; this diagnostic cannot declare a repair or relax old gates.


## Loss geometry result and matched allocation-supervision control

`effect-resolution-loss-geometry-2026-10-15` completes without fitting or parameter updates. Independent audit verifies 28,967 hashes, 16 original train/test endpoints, 46 training inputs, three explicit softmax Jacobians and all 48 card-gradient Gram matrices. Identity `00fb6988af810b760d5434f4fd1a81ac1d8692c65515c83dbaf7dbcbefaca815`. In seven of eight augmented46 endpoints, Bull/rest LP-MSE gradient cosine is negative (−.66 to −.82); combined seed1731 is positive (.715). With share-MSE at those same frozen parameters, all eight cosines become positive (.155–.421). Allocation-CE is positive in six of eight, with identity still slightly negative. These are local hypothetical gradients, not evidence of successful retraining.

The loss also weights equal allocation errors by squared attack gap: an 800-LP gap gets 16 times the LP-MSE weight of a 200-LP gap. Bull's greater-route training predictions retain roughly 7–12 LP MAE. Its allocation-CE logit-gradient norm is 18–80 times share-MSE at these rows, consistent with weaker squared-error gradients near the zero-damage simplex boundary. This magnitude ratio alone does not establish a better optimizer or causal source of transfer error. The combined observations justify a matched objective comparison, not more hand-selected data or threshold relaxation.

Preregister `effect-resolution-allocation-loss-control-2026-10-15`: three objectives × four representations × two fixed seeds = 24 fits, zero new native paths, 1200 seconds. Keep all 46 training inputs/empirical targets, the 912,582-parameter LeakyReLU(.01) allocator, initialization, normalization, masks, 1536 full-batch Adam steps and learning rate .001 identical. Retrain eight LP-MSE controls using the exact previous implementation and require bit-identical final parameters/train/test predictions. Two alternatives use the same 40 nonzero-gap rows: mean squared error of the two recipient allocation shares, or cross-entropy over attacker/defender/undelivered allocation. The six zero-gap rows contribute zero original LP gradient and are omitted from share objectives. Preserve empirical Sasuke labels and only correct floating-point simplex roundoff within 1e-6. No early stopping, optimizer tuning or candidate selection.

Freeze all endpoints before evaluation on the same 126 old groups (16 repair/110 retention) and 168 now-exposed transfer windows. Preserve both earlier overall gates, requiring both seeds, Bull/Hayabusa LP MAE <=25, deterministic/control <=100, no >10 worsening versus original/Leaky40 on registered subsets, and frozen categorical accuracy >=.95. Additionally require no >10 deterministic/control LP MAE worsening versus matched LP-MSE on old retention/opponent/order and new pooled/opponent subsets; require new Sasuke normalized LP-MSE <=1.1 times matched LP-MSE pooled/per opponent. Report paired 32-core intervals descriptively. Text benefit separately requires a full repair pass and >=10% reductions in both old retention LP MAE and new deterministic/control LP MAE versus the same-objective identity models in both seeds. These are exposed-data development comparisons. A successful new model requires fresh confirmation; all prior failures remain recorded.

## Frozen magnitude confirmation fails on stochastic lesser-than transfer

`effect-resolution-magnitude-confirmation-2026-10-15` completes 180 healthy paths, 168 reconciled windows, four historical controls and eight cold replays. Independent audit verifies 29,082 hashes, 64 LP/8 joint endpoints, six vectors, all gates and paired 32-core intervals. Identity `1ea21ef171a90c2029a011ae770aed8fd9434da406d4b6ebd67b231793d3c116`. However all eight fail stochastic nonregression, both pooled and against Judge Man: per-opponent MSE is 12.8–27.3% above full LP-MSE. Against Haniwa the stochastic gate passes. No representation or text advantage confirms; the previous development pass does not supersede this failure.

In 32 paired cores, Sasuke succeeds in 12 and fails in 20 against both defenders (empirical damage allocation .625). Against Judge Man, masked share-MSE predicts only .104–.137 damage allocation to self, versus .189–.228 from full LP-MSE. Inspection of the unchanged 46 training rows finds a possible sparse-label shortcut: the lesser-than Sasuke groups against Zera and Blue-Eyes have empirical damage share .5, while both Celtic Guardian groups have zero. Masked share-MSE fits these training targets closely, including approximately .007 at Celtic for identity seed1730. These zero labels came from few engine cores, with suffix/private-world replicates; they are not evidence that Celtic changes the coin rule. This is a hypothesis for the failed transfer, not a proven causal repair.

Preregister `effect-resolution-training-sampling-audit-2026-10-15`: freeze all 64 LP endpoints and eight joint classifiers; resample only Sasuke against the three original training defenders (66073051,89631139,91152256), preserving original requests/spec IDs except new engine seeds 2026102050–2026102081 and suffix 2026102082. Both Celtic cores repeat a coin with successful calls in both original suffixes. The new audit keeps one fixed suffix and records coin/call/damage separately across 32 engine cores. No theoretical .5 target is inserted, and no training label is changed in this study. The new evidence is a training-label diagnostic, not fresh validation. Any proposed repair still requires controlled retraining and an independent confirmation; all failed gates remain failed.
